1. **Material Imbalance:** White is currently down significant material (two Rooks for a Bishop). 
2. **Tactical Constraints:** The White Bishop on `f1` is absolutely pinned to the King on `g1` by the Black Queen on `d1`. 
3. **Defense of f1:** The White Queen on `f6` is the sole defender of the `f1` Bishop. If White plays a non-checking move that leaves the f-file (such as `1. Qxh6?`), Black has the winning defensive resource `1... Re6!`, forcing the White Queen away and subsequently winning the `f1` Bishop via `...Rxf1+` since `Kxf1` is illegal due to the Black Queen's control over the `f1` square.

Therefore, White's only path to save the game is to initiate a perpetual check starting with **1. Qg5+**. Following `1... Kh8 2. Qf6+ Kg8 3. Qg5+`, the game will end in a draw by three-fold repetition.
